Of how to create an agentic data retrieval thing or a pipeline for this specific challenge. here is context: back to db, what exactly is the time series data here? what is time series data exactly? explain me like im dumb

Explaining time-series data with a simple rail example.
Explaining time-series data with a simple rail example.
Time series, plainly

A time series is one thing measured again and again, with a clock attached. That's it.

A thermometer read every 10 minutes: 18.2, 18.4, 18.9, …
Your step count, one number per day
A heart monitor, 500 numbers per second
Three properties matter: it's the same quantity each time, the readings are in order, and the spacing is known (every minute, every day). A list of numbers with a clock is a time series; a spreadsheet of unrelated facts is not.

What the DB data is at first

A table of events. One row per train per stop: train number, station, planned time, actual time, cancelled yes/no. Thousands of rows, each about a different train. That's a table, not a series — nothing is lined up on a clock yet.

How it becomes time series

You pick one thing and track it over time:

RE 7, arrival at Bochum, minutes late, one number per day
day:    1   2   3   4   5   6   7   8 ...
late:   2   8   0  12   3   9  15   1 ...
That row is a time series. Others you'd build the same way:

Today's RE 7 along its route: lateness at stop 1, stop 2, stop 3 … (one number per stop)
Bochum station as a whole: average lateness of all trains, one number per minute
The lift on platform 4: working (1) or broken (0), checked every 15 minutes
Weather at Bochum, every 10 minutes
Why the model wants it this way

Patterns live in the sequence, not in the rows: "late every weekday evening", "started drifting in March", "spike then recovery". The model's encoder reads sequences of numbers, the way a cardiologist reads an ECG strip — and then writes the sentence. Give it the table and it sees facts; give it the series and it sees behaviour.

The answer it learns from

One more series, yes/no per day: did the connection hold? Computed from the same table (actual arrival of train A + walking time ≤ actual departure of train B). That's the label the model is trained to predict and explain.

What are the different types of data columns which we get from DB? All the different types

Read a page, searched the web
Grouped by source, with the role each column plays for you (series, annotation, or lookup).

1. Historical stop events (piebro / Bahn-Vorhersage) — your core

The piebro monthly files carry 17 columns per train stop: station_name, xml_station_name, eva (unique station id), train_name, train_number, line_number, final_destination_station, delay_in_min, time (actual arrival or departure), is_canceled, train_type (ICE, IC, RE…), train_line_ride_id (one id per run), train_line_station_num (position along the route), arrival_planned_time, arrival_change_time, departure_planned_time, departure_change_time, and a stop id. Sample rows show train_type also covers S-Bahn, regional buses and EV (replacement services), not just trains.

Series come from: delay_in_min, planned vs changed times, is_canceled — lined up per train per day, or per stop along a ride via train_line_station_num
Static annotations: train_type, line_number, final destination, eva
Bahn-Vorhersage adds the same core plus, since September 2023, the sequence of DB's own delay forecasts per stop at one-minute resolution (the forecast-evolution series)
2. Live Timetables API (IRIS) — what the archives were built from

Trip label: category (ICE/RE/S), number, operator, flags (long-distance / regional / S-Bahn)
Arrival and departure blocks: planned time, planned platform, planned path (all stops), line; changed time, changed platform, changed path, changed status (cancelled/added), cancellation time, hidden flag, wing-train links
Messages: numeric cause code (e.g., preceding-train delay, construction, signalling failure, staff shortage), message type, validity from/to, priority, category, sometimes free text — your real text annotations
Only the next ~18 hours; history must be archived (piebro's raw parquet keeps the raw XML)
3. Station master data (StaDa) — lookups

Station number, EVA ids, category 1–7, coordinates, state, and facility flags: stepless access (yes/partial/no), mobility service and its hours, toilets, lockers, parking, bicycle parking, taxi rank, WiFi, DB Lounge, travel centre, local staff, 3S emergency centre, plus Ril100 codes and platform lists.

4. Facility status (FaSta) — a live 0/1 series

Facility id, type (elevator/escalator), state (active/inactive/unknown), state explanation, location text ("zu Gleis 4"), station number, coordinates.

5. Coach sequence (Wagenreihung) — lookups

Coach order and type, platform section letters, features: wheelchair space, bike coach, quiet zone, first class, direction of travel.

6. Timetable (GTFS via DELFI/Mobilithek) — structure and accessibility

routes (route_type: rail, bus, tram, subway…), trips (wheelchair_accessible, bikes_allowed), stop_times (planned arrival/departure, stop_sequence, pickup/drop-off rules), stops (wheelchair_boarding, parent_station), transfers (min_transfer_time), calendar and calendar_dates (holidays). GTFS-RT adds per-stop delay updates and service alerts with cause, effect, active period and description text.

How they combine

Group 1 gives series and labels; group 2 gives cause text; groups 3–6 give the static facts on the journey card; group 4 is the one extra series worth adding.

do we also get gleis nummer

Checking whether platform data is included in the API.
Checking whether platform data is included in the API.
Yes, with caveats per source:

Timetables API (live): yes — planned platform and changed platform per stop (pp / cp in the XML). A platform change is literally pp ≠ cp.
Bahn-Vorhersage archive: yes — their data keeps platform per stop, so platform-change history since 2021 is there.
piebro monthly files: no — the 17 processed columns have no platform field. But the raw parquet partitions keep the original XML, so you can parse pp/cp out of them yourself.
Swiss Ist-Daten: no platform in the actual-data files; platform comes from the static stop/platform data and the journey planner, not the archive.
GTFS (DELFI): partly — stops.txt has platform_code where the operator models platform-level stops; no changes, since it's the plan.
What to do with it: derive a per-train "platform changed today" 0/1 series and a per-station "changes per day" series. For the accessibility card it matters more than the delay: a last-minute change means a different lift and a different walking route, so it should feed both the transfer-time lookup and the lift-dependence check.

 basically creating a TSLM on Deutsche Bahn historic time series data, combining with llm and telling users whether a connection by the db is actually possible or not. more context for the type of problem we are trying to solve: /Users/gaurav/Downloads/Aionic_Temporal_AI_Hackathon.pdf. check the images and what they are trying to solve with time series. dont push anything to remote. just gimme idea of firstly only the agentic data retrieval pipeline which they talk about. the pipeline should also be able to find data similar to how big ai companies get their data, but automated and agentic pipeline. go through this: https://github.com/OpenTSLM/OpenTSLM. go through this as well and lemme know how to use timenet for the training of our db data which we wanna get. also go to the other branch in the remote, learn the structure and db-train-idea is the remote other branch. also this: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02410. just the pipeline which aligns with what timenet expects

## 5. The `db_delay` pipeline — file by file

Path root: `/Users/gaurav/Downloads/zurich_lates/temporal_ai_error404.ai` → `app/agents/src/features/db_delay/` on `origin/db-train-idea`.

### `README.md` (full text, 39 lines)

```
# db_delay
German rail delay as a time-series language modelling task.

extract.py       monthly parquet over HTTPS -&gt; hourly per-station table (DuckDB)
windows.py       hourly table -&gt; context windows + targets computed from the horizon
timef.py         windows -&gt; TimeFDataset (records, annotations, TimeNet tasks)
timef_bridge.py  any TimeFDataset -&gt; OpenTSLM training samples   &lt;- generic, not rail-specific
test_db_delay.py smoke tests on synthetic data, no network needed

Run the tests: `python test_db_delay.py`. Needs `timenet[build]`, `timenet-connectors`,
`opentslm` and `duckdb` on Python 3.12.
```
&gt; **Why per station and not per train run** — "A single run visits ten to thirty stops at irregular spacing. A patch encoder has nothing to grip there, and a baseline that lists the numbers as text would match it. A station's hourly curve is dense and strongly seasonal, so the temporal model earns its place. The measured daily cycle runs from about 2 minutes at 06:00 to about 6.5 in the evening, with a second overnight peak."
&gt;
&gt; **Why the bridge exists** — "OpenTSLM does not depend on TimeNet. Its requirements name neither, and each of its dataset classes loads its own source directly, so no path existed from a TimeF dataset into training. `timef_bridge.py` is that path and knows nothing about rail: it reads records, series and tasks, so any TimeF dataset can train an OpenTSLM model."
&gt;
&gt; **Known limits** — "One month gives too few windows for a time-based split once a context week and an embargo are removed. The station split works today; the time axis needs two or three months, which costs about ten seconds of extraction each." / "Timestamps are read as Europe/Berlin. August has no transition, so a single-month build is safe. Crossing late October needs an explicit DST fold policy." / "The six-hour target has a heavy right tail, so report median absolute error next to the mean."

### `extract.py` — data acquisition (the key reuse piece)

Reads the HuggingFace parquet **in place over HTTPS with DuckDB httpfs**, no download:

```python
BASE_URL = "https://huggingface.co/datasets/piebro/deutsche-bahn-data/resolve/main/monthly_processed_data"
def month_url(month): return f"{BASE_URL}/data-{month}.parquet"   # month = "YYYY-MM"
```
`extract(con, month, n_stations=40)` runs `INSTALL httpfs; LOAD httpfs;` then two SQL statements:
1. `top_stations` = `SELECT station_name, count(*) n … GROUP BY 1 ORDER BY n DESC LIMIT n_stations`
2. `hourly` = per `station_name` × `date_trunc('hour', time)`: `avg(delay_in_min)` → `mean_delay`, `avg(CASE WHEN arrival_is_canceled OR departure_is_canceled THEN 1.0 ELSE 0.0 END)` → `cancel_rate`, `count(*)` → `trains`.

So the **source columns actually used are**: `station_name`, `time`, `delay_in_min`, `arrival_is_canceled`, `departure_is_canceled`. Docstring: *"DuckDB prunes to the five columns this needs and fetches only the byte ranges behind them, so a month costs seconds and no local copy of the 68 GB repository."*

### `windows.py` — windowing + deterministic targets + rationales

Constants: `CONTEXT_HOURS = 168` (1 week), `HORIZON_HOURS = 24`, `STRIDE_HOURS = 24`, `FORECAST_HOURS = 6`, `MAX_IMPUTED_FRACTION = 0.10`, `CHANNELS = ("mean_delay", "cancel_rate", "trains")`.

`@dataclass(frozen=True) Window` fields: `station, start_index, context: dict[str, np.ndarray], horizon_delay, imputed_fraction, next_6h_mean_delay, worse_than_context, peak_hour_of_next_day, context_mean_delay, horizon_mean_delay, hour_of_day_at_start`.

- `impute(values, present, fill)` — `"zero"` for the count channel (`trains`), `np.interp` for rate-like channels.
- `build_windows()` slides `(168 ctx, 24 horizon)` with stride 24, skipping any window whose combined imputed fraction &gt; 10%. Targets computed from the horizon: `next_6h_mean_delay = horizon_delay[:6].mean()`, `worse_than_context = horizon_mean &gt; context_mean`, `peak_hour_of_next_day = (start + 168 + argmax(horizon_delay)) % 24`.
- Three rationale writers (`rationale_for_forecast`, `rationale_for_comparison`, `rationale_for_peak`) generate chain-of-thought text that is derived from the target, so it can never contradict it. The forecast one computes a *seasonal* baseline: the mean over the context week of the same hours-of-day the horizon covers, and emits "above"/"below"/"close to" against ±15% bands.

### `timef.py` — TimeNet/TimeF dataset construction

Imports: `from timenet.dataset import TimeFDataset, TimeSeries`, `from timenet.dataset.axis import RegularAxis`, `from timenet.types import Annotation, ClassificationTask, DataSource, DatasetMetadata, ScalarPredictionTask, TimeSeriesSpec, ureg`.

- `HOURLY = RegularAxis.from_rate_hz(Fraction(1, 3600))`, `TZ = ZoneInfo("Europe/Berlin")`.
- `SOURCE = DataSource(data_source_type="operational-record", name="Deutsche Bahn timetable scrape", provider="piebro/deutsche-bahn-data")`.
- `SPECS`: `mean_delay` → `TimeSeriesSpec(spec_type="delay", unit=ureg.minute)`, `cancel_rate` → `spec_type="cancellation-rate"`, dimensionless, `trains` → `spec_type="throughput"`, dimensionless.
- `metadata()` → `DatasetMetadata(dataset_id="error404/db-delay-hourly", dataset_version="0.1.0", name="German rail hourly station delay", license="CC-BY-4.0", domains=("transport",), tags=("rail","delay","forecasting","qa"), source_url="https://huggingface.co/datasets/piebro/deutsche-bahn-data")`.
- `grids_from_duckdb(con, month_start, n_hours)` materialises the whole `hourly` table into memory, buckets by station, lays each on a complete hourly grid with a `present` mask, imputes, yields `(station, filled, present)`.
- `build()` makes one **record per window**, `record_id = f"{station-slug-lowercased}-{start_index:04d}"`, three `TimeSeries.from_values(... astype(np.float32))`, `subject_ids=(station,)`, annotations `station`, `imputed_fraction`, `context_hours`, and **three tasks per record**:
  - `ScalarPredictionTask(prompt="What is the mean delay in minutes at {station} over the next six hours?", target=round(next_6h,3), unit="minute", target_name="next_6h_mean_delay", rationale=…, id=f"{slug}-forecast")`
  - `ClassificationTask(prompt="Will the next day at {station} be worse than the past week's average?", target="worse"|"not_worse", target_schema="worse|not_worse", id=f"{slug}-comparison")`
  - `ClassificationTask(prompt="At which hour of the coming day does delay peak at {station}?", target=f"{hh:02d}", target_schema="00..23", id=f"{slug}-peak")`

Note the deviation from the idea log: the comparison target is "worse than the **context week's** mean", not the "trailing 4-week average" from the idea line.

### `timef_bridge.py` — the generic TimeF → OpenTSLM adapter

Imports `from opentslm.prompt.text_time_series_prompt import TextTimeSeriesPrompt` and `from opentslm.time_series_datasets.QADataset import QADataset`.

- `rows_from_timef(dataset, assign_split)` flattens to **one row per (task, record)** with keys `record_id, subjects, series [(signal, np.ndarray)], spec_names, units, prompt, target, rationale, task_type`, bucketed into `train`/`validation`/`test`. Docstring: *"This is where leakage control lives: hold stations and time ranges apart here, not downstream."*
- `class TimeFQADataset(QADataset)` with a class-level `SPLITS` dict; `_get_pre_prompt` ("You are given hourly measurements for {subject} covering one week, in this order: {channels}. Each series is normalised; its mean and standard deviation are given so you can recover real units."), `_get_post_prompt` (`"{prompt}\nRationale:"`), `_get_answer` (`"{rationale} Answer: {target}"`), `_get_text_time_series_prompt_list` (z-normalise per series, `MIN_STD = 1e-6`, emit `"{label}, it has mean {mean:.4f} and std {std:.4f}:"` + normalised list, raising on non-finite).

### `prepare.py` — multi-month gather + 2×2 leakage split

```python
MONTHS = ("2026-06", "2026-07", "2026-08"); FIRST_HOUR = dt.datetime(2026, 6, 1)
N_HOURS = (30 + 31 + 31) * 24; N_STATIONS = 40
HOLDOUT_EVERY = 5          # every fifth station is never seen in training
TRAIN_ENDS_AT_HOUR = 1560  # windows must finish by here to count as training
EMBARGO_HOURS = CONTEXT_HOURS   # 168h embargo
```
- `gather()` ranks stations **once on the most recent month** and pins that set into a `keep` table before looping over the earlier months — the inline comment explains why: *"extract() rebuilds top_stations each call … a station set that drifts per month would leave whole months missing per station."*
- `cell_of(record)` → `(station_is_holdout, "early"|"late"|"embargo")`.
- `assign()` drops embargo rows; holdout stations and late-period windows → `test`; of the seen/early rows, `index % 13 == 0` → `test` (so the memorization ceiling cell is measurable), `index % 11 == 0` → `validation`, else `train`.
- Emits four eval cells `seen_early / seen_late / unseen_early / unseen_late` and pickles `{"buckets", "cells", "holdout"}` to `&lt;out_dir&gt;/splits.pkl`. CLI: `python prepare.py [out_dir=data]`.
- Uses a module-level mutable `HOLDOUT: set[str]` with a `global` write — fragile but functional.

### `train_minimal.py` — the OpenTSLM fine-tune

Imports `from opentslm.model.llm.OpenTSLMSP import OpenTSLMSP`, `from opentslm.model_config import PATCH_SIZE`, `from opentslm.time_series_datasets.util import extend_time_series_to_match_patch_size_and_aggregate`, torch, and `TimeFQADataset`.

CLI defaults: `--data data/splits.pkl`, `--out out`, `--llm-id google/gemma-3-270m`, `--max-steps 400`, `--batch-size 8`, `--eval-batch-size 8`, `--eval-limit 96`, `--lr 2e-4`, `--lora` (flag), `--max-new-tokens 120`.

Flow: load pickle → `device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"` → `OpenTSLMSP(llm_id, device)` → optional `model.enable_lora()` with the comment *"Without this the language model is frozen and never learns the answer format, so it emits fluent text that no scorer can parse."* → `AdamW(trainable, lr, weight_decay=1e-2)` with `clip_grad_norm_(…, 1.0)` → a **pre-training eval on `unseen_late`** as the before-baseline → step loop printing loss every 25 steps → per-cell generate+score → writes `out/predictions_&lt;cell&gt;.jsonl`, `out/metrics.json`, and `out/checkpoint.pt` via `model.store_to_file`.

Scoring (`score()`): scalar tasks get MAE + median AE vs a **persistence baseline = mean of the context `mean_delay` channel**; classification gets accuracy vs the **majority-class baseline**; answers parsed with `re.compile(r"Answer:\s*([-\w.]+)")`, unparseable scalar counts as predicting zero.

### `rescore.py` — offline per-task rescoring

No GPU, no regeneration: reads `predictions_&lt;cell&gt;.jsonl` from a run dir, splits the two classification tasks apart (peak-hour identified by `^\d{2}$` on the target), reports `n`, `parse_rate`, `mae`/`median_ae` or `accuracy`/`majority_baseline`/`accuracy_when_parsed`, lifts `baseline_mae`/`baseline_median_ae` out of `metrics.json`, and writes `metrics_per_task.json`. CLI: `python rescore.py [run_dir=out/run2]` — the `out/run2` default implies at least two real runs happened locally (no artifacts are committed).

### `test_db_delay.py` — seven offline tests

Builds a synthetic in-memory DuckDB `hourly` table (6 stations × 744 hours, `3 + 2*sin(2πh/24)` plus noise, a scattered gap every 97 hours) and asserts: records+tasks exist and `len(tasks) == 3 * len(records)`; each record has 3 finite channels of exactly 168 samples; horizon never exceeds the month; **no station crosses the split**; formatted samples carry `pre_prompt`/3 series/`Answer:`/EOS; series text states mean and std; and `test_forecast_rationale_agrees_with_its_answer` parses the rationale's seasonal number back out and asserts direction words match the target. `sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent))` at import time.

Python `&gt;=3.10` declared here vs the db_delay README's *"Needs … on Python 3.12"* — mismatch to resolve. `app/agents/README.md` suggests `pip install -e .` then `python -m agents.main` as the eventual entrypoint. Feature-folder convention: one folder per capability under `src/features/`, name-matched 1:1 with the backend/frontend halves.

**`app/frontend/` is a pure placeholder** — `package.json` carries `"_hackos_placeholder": true` and echo-only `dev`/`build` scripts; only `_TEMPLATE` dirs exist.

`app/README.md` states the layering rule: *"Everything under `../team-setup/` is coordination, not code — this is where the hackathon build lives."* and *"Inside each of `frontend/`, `backend/`, `agents/`: one folder per feature under `src/features/`, not a `components/`/`routes/`/`models/` split at the top level."* `app/shared/` is generated from the contract and **never hand-edited**.

### `app/backend`

Node ≥ 22, **zero npm dependencies** (`http`, `fetch`, `crypto` built-ins only). Scripts: `dev` (`node --watch src/server.js`), `start`, `test` (`node --test "src/**/*.test.js"`), `train` (`node src/features/training-jobs/cli.js`), `train:doctor`, `train:smoke`. `"type": "module"`.

`src/server.js` is ~80 lines: a hand-rolled `Router` class (`add/get/post/delete`, `:param` → regex capture), `HttpError`, `readJson`, `sendJson`, `loadDotenv` via `process.loadEnvFile`, and `createApp({env})` which registers `GET /api/health` and then `registerTrainingJobs(router, {env})`. Adding a feature = export a `registerRoutes(router, ctx)` and add one import line in `server.js`.

### `app/backend/src/features/training-jobs/` — Nebius job spec, exact shape

**Auth (`nebius/auth.js`)** — three strategies tried in order inside `createTokenProvider(env)`, with in-memory caching until `expiry - 120s`:
1. `NEBIUS_ACCESS_TOKEN` (static, 12 h, from `nebius iam get-access-token`)
2. `NEBIUS_SA_ID` + `NEBIUS_SA_KEY_ID` + `NEBIUS_SA_PRIVATE_KEY_FILE|NEBIUS_SA_PRIVATE_KEY` → RS256 JWT (`kid` header, `iss == sub == saId`, 300 s TTL) exchanged at `https://auth.eu.nebius.com/oauth2/token/exchange` via RFC 8693 form grant.
3. Fallback: shell out to the Nebius CLI (`$NEBIUS_CLI` → `PATH` → `~/.nebius/bin/nebius`) `iam get-access-token`.

**Client (`nebius/client.js`)** — `API_BASE_URL = 'https://api.nebius.cloud'`; endpoints `POST /ai/v1/jobs` (returns an Operation; `jobId = op.resourceId`), `GET /ai/v1/jobs/{id}`, `GET /ai/v1/jobs/get-by-name`, `GET /ai/v1/jobs?parentId=…`, `POST /ai/v1/jobs/cancel`, `POST /ai/v1/jobs/restart`, `DELETE /ai/v1/jobs/{id}`, plus `GET /iam/v1/profiles` and `GET /vpc/v1/subnets`. States: `PROVISIONING, STARTING, IMAGE_PULLING, RUNNING, CANCELLING, DELETING, COMPLETED, FAILED, CANCELLED, ERROR`; terminal = last four. `waitForJob` polls every 15 s with an `onUpdate` callback. `summarizeJob` is the compact UI view.

**Spec (`nebius/spec.js`)** — `buildJobRequest(input, env)` produces exactly:

```js
{
  metadata: { parentId: NEBIUS_PROJECT_ID, name, labels? },
  spec: {
    image,                                  // default pytorch/pytorch:2.7.1-cuda12.8-cudnn9-runtime
    containerCommand?,                      // input.command
    args?,                                  // single string
    platform, preset,                       // default gpu-l40s-a / 1gpu-8vcpu-32gb
    timeout: "&lt;seconds&gt;s",                  // 1h..168h enforced
    disk: { type: 'NETWORK_SSD', sizeBytes: String(diskGiB * 1024**3) },   // default 250 GiB
    shmSizeBytes: String(shmGiB * 1024**3), // default 16 GiB
    subnetId?, workingDir?, preemptible?, publicIp?, restartAttempts?,
    environmentVariables?: [{ name, value } | { name, mysteryboxSecret }],
    volumes?: [{ source, containerPath, mode: 'READ_WRITE'|'READ_ONLY', sourcePath? }],
    injectedFiles?: [{ containerPath, content }],   // 64 KiB each, hard limit
    sshAuthorizedKeys?, registryCredentials?: { username, password },
  },
  dryRun?: true
}
```
Validation: job name must match `/^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]{0,61}[a-z0-9])?$/`; preset must be in the platform's catalog list; volume `containerPath` must be absolute; volume strings parse as `SOURCE:/container/path[:ro|rw]` with special handling for `s3://bucket:/path:mode`. `estimateCost` multiplies `pricePerGpuHour × gpusInPreset(preset) × hours` (×0.5 assumed for preemptible).

Platform catalog with $/GPU-hour: `gpu-l40s-a` 1.55 (L40S 48 GB, AMD host, presets `1gpu-8vcpu-32gb` … `1gpu-40vcpu-160gb`), `gpu-l40s-d` 1.82 (Intel host, up to `4gpu-192vcpu-1152gb`), `gpu-h100-sxm` 3.85 (`1gpu-16vcpu-200gb`, `8gpu-128vcpu-1600gb`), `gpu-h200-sxm` 4.50, `gpu-b200-sxm` 7.15.

**How code + data reach the job** — `buildTrainingJobRequest(input, env)` is the "run this Python script on a GPU" convenience:
- injects `{workdir}/{scriptName}` (default `/workspace/train.py`) from `script` text or `scriptPath`
- injects `{workdir}/requirements.txt` if given
- generates and injects `{workdir}/run.sh`:
  ```bash
  #!/usr/bin/env bash
  set -euo pipefail
  cd /workspace
  nvidia-smi || true
  &lt;input.setup if any&gt;
  pip install --no-cache-dir -q -r requirements.txt
  exec python train.py &lt;shell-quoted scriptArgs&gt;
  ```
  with the comment *"The API's `args` is one string whose tokenisation rules are not documented, so never rely on quoting inside it: inject a run.sh and execute that."*
- sets `command: 'bash'`, `args: '/workspace/run.sh'`, `workingDir: '/workspace'`
- if `outputVolume` (or `NEBIUS_OUTPUT_VOLUME`) is set, appends `{source: outputVolume, containerPath: '/outputs', mode: 'rw'}` and injects `NEBIUS_OUTPUT_DIR=/outputs` into the env.

The README's rule for data, verbatim: *"**Small code/config (≤ 64 KiB each):** inject it (`--script`, `--requirements`, `injectedFiles`). **Datasets, checkpoints, outputs:** create an Object Storage bucket in the console (**Storage → Object Storage → Create bucket**) and mount it: `--volume my-bucket:/data` or `--output my-bucket`. The container disk is thrown away when the job ends. **Hugging Face Hub:** pass `--env HF_TOKEN=…` and `push_to_hub` from the script. **Private images:** `registry: {username, password}` in the JSON spec."*

**Service + routes (`routes/training-jobs.js`)** — `createTrainingService({env, client, fetchImpl})` exposes `catalog, estimate, whoami, build, submit, dryRun, list, get, wait, cancel, remove, logs`. `build()` dispatches: `body.script || body.scriptPath` → `buildTrainingJobRequest`, else `buildJobRequest`. Responses `redact()` injected file contents down to `{containerPath, bytes}` and mask registry passwords. Logs are **CLI-only** (Nebius has no logs REST endpoint) — returns HTTP 501 if the CLI is missing. HTTP surface: `GET /api/training/{catalog,estimate,whoami,jobs,jobs/:id,jobs/:id/logs}`, `POST /api/training/{jobs,jobs/dry-run,jobs/:id/cancel}`, `DELETE /api/training/jobs/:id`.

**CLI (`cli.js`)** — `doctor` (auth + project + subnet discovery, prints the `NEBIUS_SUBNET_ID` line to paste), `catalog`, `estimate`, `smoke` (nvidia-smi on 1× L40S, ~$0.10, 3–6 min), `submit` (`--script --requirements --platform --preset --timeout --image --env K=V --volume SRC:/path[:ro] --output BUCKET --disk-gib --preemptible --setup --dry-run --wait -- &lt;script args&gt;`), `submit-spec &lt;file.json&gt;`, `list`, `status/get`, `wait`, `logs [--follow --tail --since]`, `cancel`, `delete`.

**Env (`app/backend/.env.example`)** — `NEBIUS_PROJECT_ID` (required), `NEBIUS_SUBNET_ID`, one of the three auth modes, plus defaults `REDACTED`, `REDACTED`, `NEBIUS_DEFAULT_IMAGE=pytorch/pytorch:2.7.1-cuda12.8-cudnn9-runtime`, `NEBIUS_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT=2h`, `NEBIUS_DEFAULT_DISK_GIB=250`, `NEBIUS_OUTPUT_VOLUME`, `PORT=8000`, `NEBIUS_CLI`.

**Tests (`training-jobs.test.js`)** — 12 offline `node --test` cases covering duration parsing, quick-start request shape, validation, env/volume mapping, run.sh injection, cost scaling, JWT signing/verification, RFC 8693 exchange + caching, bearer headers and endpoint paths, `waitForJob` polling, and route redaction. Budget table in the README says $600 buys ~385 L40S GPU-hours at $1.55/h (ideas.md says the voucher is $1000).

## 7. Constraints from `AGENTS.md` on `origin/main`

`AGENTS.md` is **byte-identical on `origin/main` and `origin/db-train-idea`**. `CLAUDE.md` just points at it. Every rule that constrains adding code:

**§1 Boot / freshness** — Read `event.json` first; `mode: example`, a `WORKED EXAMPLE` `_comment`, or a blank `event_name` all mean no active event (here `mode: active`, `event_name: "Temporal AI"` → active). For an active event read in this order: `pipeline.json` → `decisions.md` → `owners.md` → `board/tasks.json` → `challenge.md` → `demo-script.md` → `not-building.md` → `contract.md`. *"State the active stage, your confirmed ownership, current task/decisions, and whether the real contract is frozen. Missing information is unknown, not permission to invent assignments or implement fictional requirements."* Git is explicit synchronization: `event.json` names the coordination branch (`main`) and maintainer (`Herit`); teammates fetch and integrate before new work; *"Never auto-pull into a dirty worktree, discard changes, auto-stash, or resolve another person's conflicts."*

**§2 Hackathon mode** — verbatim:
&gt; - Write smoke tests for the demo happy path and coordination data safety.
&gt; - Do not add unit tests for unrelated product code or refactor working code.
&gt; - No speculative abstractions, new dashboards, providers, or services.
&gt; - Core storage/API stays Python 3 stdlib plus Git. The dashboard UI uses Vite, React, Tailwind and shadcn/ui in `team-setup/board/web/`; its Node dependencies are separate from whichever product stack the team chooses.
&gt; - Optional MCPs, plugins, prompt logging, and agent teams are not prerequisites.
&gt; - Vendored skills are optional; these repository rules override their workflow defaults. A skill does not authorize unrequested installs, data uploads, extra agents, destructive commands, or changes to system security controls.

Important scoping detail: the "Python 3 stdlib plus Git" rule applies to **hack-os's own core storage/API** (i.e. `team-setup/board/`), *not* to product code under `app/` — the file opens with *"These rules govern product work; explicitly requested maintenance of hack-os itself is not constrained by an example product's demo script or contract"*, and `app/README.md`/`app/agents/pyproject.toml` explicitly leave the product stack to the team. Third-party Python (duckdb, torch, opentslm, timenet) under `app/agents/` is therefore allowed, but it must be declared in `app/agents/pyproject.toml` with the `_hackos_placeholder` comment removed, because `bootstrap.sh` gates installs on that marker.

**§3 Ownership** — *"Before editing, check `team-setup/state/owners.md` and the owning task in `team-setup/board/tasks.json`. Do not edit another person's assigned files."* Name owners for shared wiring, manifests and lockfiles too. *"Use one branch/worktree per independent writer."* Right now `owners.md` is entirely blank and `tasks.json` is empty, so `app/agents` has **no recorded owner** — claim it explicitly before writing there.

**§4 Contract** — the real `contract.md` freezes at that gate; code against it and mock missing dependencies; a change needs human approval + a recorded decision; `app/shared/` is generated, never hand-patched. Contract is currently **empty/unfrozen** (stage is still `challenge-pick`), so nothing constrains interfaces yet — and nothing legitimises them either.

**§5 Scope** — product work must support the real demo script; ask before adding features absent from it; maintain `not-building.md`; the planning window's final 10% is buffer.

**§6 Idea rule** — *"A thought is not a decision."* New ideas append as `- [i-&lt;uuid&gt;] [T+HH:MM] &lt;idea&gt; — &lt;raised by&gt; — status: raised`; only the status changes in place, original text and author stay.

**§7 Decision rule** — append to `decisions.md` as `- [d-&lt;uuid&gt;] [T+HH:MM] &lt;decision&gt; — &lt;who decided&gt; — &lt;reason&gt;`; never rewrite; ` — supersedes [d-&lt;uuid&gt;]`, ` — promotes [i-&lt;uuid&gt;]`.

**§8 Evidence and safety** — AI assessments advisory, keep sources; *"Task completion is not integrated readiness: require a current end-to-end smoke result."* Keep secrets and runtime caches out of Git.

**§9 Commits and verification** — *"Small present-tense commits identify the demo step or coordination outcome. Never commit or push unless requested. Do not amend other people's work."* Required checks: `python3 team-setup/board/test_board.py`; `for f in bootstrap.sh team-setup/hooks/* team-setup/scripts/*.sh; do bash -n "$f" || exit 1; done`; `node --check team-setup/board/app.js`; `npm --prefix team-setup/board/web run build`; product checks via `team-setup/scripts/smoke.sh` (*"unconfigured is not a pass"*). `smoke.sh` currently defaults `SMOKE_COMMAND` to `npm --prefix app/backend test --silent` and has no health URL — so a Python pipeline needs to be wired into that EDIT-ME block to count.

## 8. Gaps / risks for a data-pipeline design

- **No declared dependencies.** `app/agents/pyproject.toml` is untouched on `db-train-idea`; `duckdb`, `numpy`, `torch`, `opentslm`, `timenet[build]`, `timenet-connectors` are imported but nowhere declared. The `_hackos_placeholder` comment must be deleted for `bootstrap.sh` to install anything. Python-version conflict: `requires-python = "&gt;=3.10"` vs README's "Python 3.12".
- **Flat intra-package imports.** `prepare.py` does `from extract import extract` / `from timef import build`, and the test does `sys.path.insert(0, Path(__file__).parent)`. The modules only work with cwd (or sys.path) set to the feature directory — they are not importable as `app.agents.src.features.db_delay.*`. This will bite when packaging code for a Nebius job.
- **64 KiB injection limit vs this pipeline.** The five runtime modules total roughly 25 KB of source, so they *could* each be injected as separate `injectedFiles` entries; but `spec.buildTrainingJobRequest` only injects one script + requirements + run.sh. Either extend it to take multiple files, or push the package plus `splits.pkl` to an Object Storage bucket and mount it (`--volume bucket:/data`). Note `extract.py` reads HuggingFace over HTTPS from inside the container, which works from a Nebius job as long as egress is allowed — that avoids staging the parquet entirely.
- **`splits.pkl` transfer.** `train_minimal.py` expects a local pickle; nothing yet uploads it. Either run `prepare.py` inside the job (add it to `run.sh` via `--setup`) or mount a bucket.
- **`not-building.md` says the base model stays frozen; `train_minimal.py --lora` unfreezes it.** Reconcile before the cut list is frozen.
- **Zero decisions, zero owners, zero tasks, empty challenge.md/contract.md, empty rubric.** Per AGENTS.md §1/§3/§4 none of the DB direction is authorised scope yet — it is all `status: raised`/`exploring` in `ideas.md`. Adopting it requires a `[d-&lt;uuid&gt;] … — promotes [i-&lt;uuid&gt;]` line and an `owners.md` entry for `app/agents`.
- **No frontend/backend half for `db_delay`.** `app/README.md` requires 1:1 feature names across layers where a feature spans them; there is no `app/backend/src/features/db_delay/` nor a frontend counterpart, and nothing wires the pipeline to the Nebius submitter.</result>
