# 0. What changed

| | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Codebase | layer on `db_delay` | **standalone** `app/agents/src/features/db_route/`, own branch, zero imports from his modules |
| Convergence | shared modules | **only at the TimeNet boundary** — both emit a valid `TimeFDataset`; we also cross-load *his* dataset through *our* bridge as an interoperability proof |
| Output | one dataset | **a profile-parameterized pipeline emitting 4 sibling datasets**, bake-off on identical held-out cells |
| Headline claim | "route target on dense series" | "route target on dense series, **and we measured which history horizon actually carries the signal**" |

The bake-off is the better story. "We built a dataset" is a deliverable; "we built four and let the held-out cells choose" is a result.

---

# 1. Standalone architecture

Branch `feat/db-route-multiscale`, directory `app/agents/src/features/db_route/`. The DuckDB-`httpfs`-over-HTTPS read of the HF parquet is a public technique and we use it; nothing else is borrowed.

```
sources.py       remote parquet URLs + the one httpfs read helper
facts.py         download side: builds the FACT CACHE (see §3.4)
profiles.py      TimelineProfile / Layer config objects  &lt;- the pivot of the whole design
resample.py      ONE parameterized re-bucketing path, fact cache -&gt; any profile's grids
itineraries.py   mine (L1, X, L2) connection templates from observed runs
labels.py        horizon labels + context slot dicts (labels are PROFILE-INDEPENDENT)
splits.py        the shared holdout artifact, computed once for all profiles
rationale.py     slot dict -&gt; skeleton fill (skeletons shipped as committed JSON)
build.py         profile -&gt; TimeFDataset
bridge.py        our TimeF -&gt; OpenTSLM adapter, with the caption fix
validate.py      fail-closed validator, incl. cross-profile identity assertions
bakeoff.py       identical train+eval harness across profiles; emits the results table
```

Connector packaging: **four folders**, each ~10 lines over a shared internal module:
`datasets/error404/db-route-{week,quarter,year,multiscale}/{__init__.py, connector.py, dataset.yaml, requirements.txt, tests/}`, each ending `CONNECTOR = …`. `dataset.yaml` is `additionalProperties: false`, so the profile is encoded in `name`, `description` and `tags` (`profile:S`, `bucket:1h`, `span:7d`) — **do not invent a top-level `profile:` key, the card will fail schema validation.**

**Interoperability proof, cheap and high-value:** load the teammate's `error404/db-delay-hourly` through *our* `bridge.py` and train one run on it. Two independently written pipelines meeting at the format, plus a bridge that doesn't care which produced the dataset, is the strongest possible evidence for the reusable-connector bonus — stronger than one pipeline, because it shows the boundary actually holds.

---

# 2. The record shape, resolved once

Resolved as before, and now it must hold at every timescale — which it does, because the profile changes only *which series* hang off the record, never the record itself.

**Fork resolution (unchanged):** a route is legs; legs are grounded in dense aggregates; the teammate's density argument is preserved because **run-level data becomes the label, not the series.** The per-line curve (a service group aggregated across all its stops) is as dense and seasonal as a per-station curve, so the route question needs no irregular per-run sequence. The destination gets no series at all — arriving late at B is the outcome, not a driver.

**Six canonical signals**, identical at every timescale:

| # | `signal` | Content | spec | unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `board_station_delay` | mean delay, all trains at boarding station **A** | `delay` | minute |
| 2 | `board_line_delay` | mean delay of service group **L1** across its stops | `delay` | minute |
| 3 | `transfer_station_delay` | mean delay at transfer hub **X** | `delay` | minute |
| 4 | `transfer_station_cancel_rate` | cancellation rate at **X** | `cancellation-rate` | dimensionless |
| 5 | `connect_line_delay` | mean delay of service group **L2** across its stops | `delay` | minute |
| 6 | `connect_line_service` | stop-event count for **L2** (service level) | `throughput` | dimensionless |

Service group = `train_type || ' → ' || final_destination_station` (always populated; `line_number` is empty for much of ICE/IC). Upgrade to `line_number` only if the H0 gate shows ≥90 % population.

**Tasks per record** (identical across profiles — this is required for the comparison):

- **Primary** `ScalarPredictionTask(target_name="required_buffer_p80_min", unit="minute")` — the smallest transfer buffer that would have held on 4 days out of 5, = `clip(p80({d1 + m − d2} over horizon instances), 0, 60)`.
- **Rider** `ClassificationTask(target_schema="book_as_planned|add_buffer|depart_earlier|avoid_transfer")` — deterministic policy over the same numbers.
- **Rider** `AnswerTask` — the prose plan. **Put STATE SUMMARY + reasoning in `rationale` and only the one-sentence action in `target`**, because the bridge builds the answer as `rationale + " Answer: " + target`.
- **Optional** `ScalarPredictionTask("realized_delay_travel_day_min")`, scored on median AE.

`rationale: str` on the Task base carries the chain of thought for all of them. Three to four tasks per record is nowhere near needing `set_task_stream`.

**The bridge defect that must be fixed in our implementation:** captions come from `spec.name`, but four of six series share the one `delay` spec — so four would be captioned identically. At XM it is worse: the same signal appears at three timescales. Our bridge reads a record annotation `caption:&lt;layer&gt;/&lt;signal&gt;` and falls back to `spec.name`. Caption text names entity *and* window:

&gt; `Mean delay of ICE → Hamburg-Altona across its stops, daily over the past year, it has mean 6.2314 and std 2.1077:`

Since the caption is the only channel for scale *and* identity, this is load-bearing, not cosmetic.

---

# 3. The timeline profiles

## 3.1 The cap arithmetic, which is why this is a tradeoff curve and not "just use more context"

OpenTSLM caps a series at **4096 samples** (1024 patches × 4). Therefore:

- hourly × 1 year = **8760 — over the cap.** Hourly tops out at 4096 h = **170 days.**
- Even at the cap, 6 series × 1024 patches = 6144 patch tokens, which is roughly where compute stops being cheap anyway. **The cap and the compute budget bite at about the same place**, so there is no "just pay for it" escape.

Span and resolution genuinely trade off. That is the premise of the whole experiment, and it is worth one slide.

## 3.2 The ladder (your sketch, refined)

I keep your M at 6-hourly/90 days rather than switching to daily/180 — it makes the family a clean monotone ladder (resolution ÷6 each rung) and it preserves coarse time-of-day structure, which matters because our target is departure-hour-specific.

| Profile | bucket | steps | span | patches/series | series | patches/sample |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **S** "operational week" | 1 h | **168** | 7 d | 42 | 6 | 252 |
| **M** "regime quarter" | 6 h | **360** | 90 d | 90 | 6 | 540 |
| **L** "seasonal year" | 1 d | **364** | 52 w | 91 | 6 | 546 |
| **XM** "multi-scale" | 1h+6h+1d | 168/360/364 | 7d+90d+52w | — | **12** | 795 |

Three refinements worth noting:

- **All three lengths are divisible by 4** (42, 90, 91 patches) — no right-zero-padding anywhere, which removes an entire class of silent bug given there is no padding mask.
- **364, not 365.** 364 = 52 × 7 exactly, so weekday phase is stable across the whole series (index `i` ↔ weekday `i mod 7`), *and* 364/4 = 91 is exact. 365 gives you neither.
- **Phase anchoring is uniform:** every layer ends at the shared anchor `t0` = 00:00 local on the travel day. So index `i` ↔ hour-of-day `i mod 24` in S, ↔ 6h block `i mod 4` in M, ↔ weekday `i mod 7` in L. Free, strong inductive bias in all three.

- **M and L stay all-hours, not time-of-day-banded.** I originally wanted band-conditioning at M, but the controlled comparison must vary exactly one thing — the (bucket, span) pair. Banding would change the *channel definition* too and confound the result. The `tod_filter` field stays in the config (it is a `WHERE` clause, costs nothing) and gets used only for an optional fifth profile `M-band`, first to be dropped.

## 3.3 XM: include it, and treat it as the hypothesis rather than a bonus

**Yes, include it — and it should be framed as the primary hypothesis, with S as the null.**

S vs M vs L is a *substitution* test: does a quarter beat a week? But nobody plans that way. Your actual stated question — *"construction which happened last month but was not there 3 months ago"* — is an **additivity** question: does long history help *on top of* the operational week? **Only XM can answer that.** S/M/L alone cannot.

Composition: all 6 signals at S, plus 3 at M (`board_line_delay`, `connect_line_delay`, `transfer_station_delay`), plus 3 at L (`transfer_station_delay`, `connect_line_delay`, `connect_line_service`) = **12 series**, exactly the top of the paper's 1–12 range. The rule for admitting a series is unchanged: *every series must be referenced by a named clause of the rationale.* The regime-trend clause needs the three M series; the season clause needs the three L series. The full-18 union is a one-line config change if there is spare GPU.

Two further arguments: XM is **free** — it is a profile with three `Layer`s, no new code; and it **exercises TimeNet's multi-rate record support** (three different `RegularAxis` rates on one record), which is a genuinely nice thing to show the organisers.

And note the honest framing: if XM does **not** beat S, that is a real, reportable negative result — "the operational week carries the signal; annual context did not add" — which is a better outcome than a mushy win. Either way you have a finding.

## 3.4 One code path: the fact cache + the profile object

The reason three profiles are not three pipelines is that **all resampling is one parameterized aggregation over a single hourly fact cache.**

`download()` (network, once) writes a cache at the **finest granularity anyone will need**, storing sums and counts rather than means:

```
hourly_station(station_name, hour, sum_delay, n_obs, n_cancelled)
hourly_line   (service_group, hour, sum_delay, n_obs, n_cancelled)
connection_instances(route_id, service_date, sched_arr, sched_dep, d1, d2, cancelled)
```

Storing `sum_delay`/`n_obs` instead of `mean_delay` is not a detail — **re-bucketing a mean-of-means across hours with different train counts is biased.** Every coarser bucket is `sum(sum_delay)/sum(n_obs)`. One SQL template covers every profile:

```sql
SELECT entity,
       time_bucket(INTERVAL &lt;bucket&gt;, hour, TIMESTAMP &lt;origin&gt;) AS b,
       sum(sum_delay)/nullif(sum(n_obs),0) AS mean_delay, ...
FROM   &lt;fact_table&gt;
WHERE  hour &gt;= &lt;t0 - span&gt; AND hour &lt; &lt;t0&gt;  [AND &lt;tod_filter&gt;]
GROUP BY 1,2
```

The config object:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Layer:
    layer_id: str            # "week" | "quarter" | "year"
    bucket: str              # "1 hour" | "6 hours" | "1 day"
    n_steps: int             # asserted divisible by 4 and &lt;= 4096
    rate_hz: Fraction        # 1/3600 | 1/21600 | 1/86400
    tod_filter: Band | None
    signals: tuple[str, ...] # subset of the six canonical signals

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TimelineProfile:
    profile_id: str          # "S" | "M" | "L" | "XM"
    dataset_id: str          # "error404/db-route-week"
    layers: tuple[Layer, ...]
```

`build.py` iterates `profile.layers × layer.signals`, emitting one `TimeSeries` per pair with `signal=f"{layer_id}/{name}"`, the layer's `RegularAxis`, and the shared per-modality spec. **XM is literally `TimelineProfile(layers=(WEEK, QUARTER, YEAR))`.** That is the whole answer to "one code path, not three."

`n_steps` being a config field is also the graceful degradation path: if the long extraction underruns, `L` becomes `1d × 180` by editing one integer, not by redesigning.

---

# 4. The labels must be identical — and the anchor set too

**The label is defined purely from the horizon and the itinerary, never from the context.** Horizon is always the 7 days following `t0`, for every profile. Therefore `labels.parquet` is computed **once**, profile-independently, and joined into all four builds. No profile can have an ill-defined target, because no profile touches the target.

The invariant to state on the slide:

&gt; **Same anchors, same labels, same splits, same tasks, same harness, same seed. The only thing that varies is how much history precedes `t0`.**

Two consequences that are easy to get wrong and would invalidate the whole bake-off:

**(a) The anchor set must be the intersection, i.e. L-limited.** L needs 364 days of prior history; S needs 7. Left alone, S would train on ~10× more anchors and win for a reason that has nothing to do with timescale. **All profiles use the anchor set that L can support.** This is the single most important methodological point here.

Report a clearly-labelled secondary row — *budget-matched*, each profile using all the anchors it can, capped to equal training-sample count — which answers the different and also-interesting practical question. It is the first extra to drop.

**(b) Anchor stride = 7 days, equal to the horizon.** Consequence: **horizons are exactly disjoint, so no two records share a single label observation.** Assign each itinerary a fixed weekday offset by `index mod 7` so the corpus covers all weekdays while any one itinerary's horizons stay disjoint.

Sizing: L-valid anchors run ~2025-06-30 → ~2026-08-24, ≈ 60 weekly anchors. × ~200 curated itineraries = **~12 000 records × 3–4 tasks ≈ 40 k samples** per profile. Ample for a few hundred LoRA steps at batch 8.

**One honest caveat, and it is intrinsic rather than a design flaw.** With weekly stride, consecutive records for one itinerary overlap in context by 0 % at S, ~92 % at M, ~98 % at L. So L has ~60 anchors but barely more than one independent context-year of information. Long contexts are *intrinsically* data-starved on a fixed calendar. Report the effective-independent-context count per profile alongside the metrics. This does not invalidate the comparison — the test cells are temporally disjoint, so any resulting overfitting shows up honestly as poor test performance — but it is the most likely reason L-alone underperforms, and predicting that in advance is more impressive than explaining it afterwards. It is also the sharpest argument for **XM**: it gets the annual level information without having to depend on it alone.

**Rationale under multiple timescales.** Context-derived slots necessarily differ per profile (L has no "at this hour" statistic). Keep the **clause structure identical** — *level / cycle / trend / risk / action* — and vary only the window each statistic is computed over: S says "over the past week, at this hour"; L says "over the past year, and over the last four weeks"; XM says both. Identical structure keeps the supervision comparable; only the evidence changes. Skeleton sets are per-profile, ~400 LLM calls total.

---

# 5. Coverage: the constraint, and the artifact it would otherwise create

piebro covers ~100 busiest stations from **2024-07**, all stations only from **2025-11-02**. L needs 364 days of context, so **L is top-100-only**, as you said.

But the consequence runs deeper than "L uses fewer stations", and this one would have quietly destroyed the M and L results:

&gt; Any aggregate whose observation basis expands on **2025-11-02** shows a step discontinuity there — a jump in observation count and very likely a level shift in mean delay, since minor stations have a different delay profile than Hbf. A line's "network-wide" mean computed over all available stops is exactly such an aggregate. An M or L series straddling 2025-11-02 would contain a manufactured regime change, and the model would dutifully learn it.

**Fix: pin the observation basis. Every aggregate — station *and* line — is restricted to the fixed top-100 station set over the entire period.** The line series becomes "mean delay of service group L *at its top-100 stops*" — a well-defined quantity on a constant basis, documented in the card. The universe itself is pinned once as `station_universe.json` (stations present continuously 2024-07 → 2026-08) and shared by all profiles, so the entity set cannot drift between them either.

Then add the corresponding validator check: for each profile, assert no step in per-bucket observation count exceeding a threshold at 2025-11-02, and assert mean-delay continuity across that date. Cheap, and it converts an invisible failure into a build error.

---

# 6. Leakage: within a profile, and across profiles

## 6.1 Within (unchanged in substance)

The teammate's per-station holdout does not transfer to routes: a route touches three stations, so "all three unseen" is an empty cell (~0.8 %) and "any one unseen" contaminates half the corpus. Line leakage is new and worse — one service group appears in dozens of itineraries. Corridor near-duplicates share 5 of 6 series.

- **Axis R:** hold out **whole transfer hubs** — every 5th hub by traffic rank is never a transfer hub in training. The hub owns series 3 and 4 *and* the label is computed there. Be explicit that a held-out hub may still appear as a boarding station: "never appears anywhere" is unachievable on a connected network, and disclosing that is better than being caught by it.
- **Axis L:** every 7th service group never appears as L1 or L2 in training; reported as a fifth diagnostic number, not a full cell.
- **Near-duplicate control:** ≤ 8 itineraries per hub; no two records sharing `(hub, line_pair, anchor)`. Without this you ship "12 000 records" that are really twelve routes.

## 6.2 The embargo has to be redefined for long contexts

The teammate's `EMBARGO_HOURS = CONTEXT_HOURS` cannot survive a 364-day context — there is no calendar room for a one-year embargo, and test contexts *must* overlap the training period. The correct statement:

&gt; **The embargo applies to the horizon, not the context.** Train records never see any data after the boundary (enforce `anchor + 7d ≤ boundary`). Test records may see data *before* the boundary — that is exactly what deployment looks like and is not leakage. Embargo = 7 days (one horizon) so no test label observation is also a train label observation.

This is airtight, easy to say in one sentence, and it is a place where the per-station design genuinely does not transfer.

## 6.3 Across profiles — the mechanical guarantee

Split assignment must be **provably** identical in all four datasets, or the comparison means nothing. Guarantee it by construction rather than by discipline: **five shared artifacts, computed once before any profile is built, each content-hashed.**

| artifact | contents |
|---|---|
| `station_universe.json` | the pinned top-100 |
| `itineraries.json` | mined + curated selection (~200) |
| `anchors.json` | L-limited weekly anchors + per-itinerary weekday offsets |
| `labels.parquet` | per (itinerary, anchor) targets and horizon stats |
| `splits.json` | holdout hubs, holdout service groups, time boundary, embargo |

`assign_split(record)` is then a pure function of `(hub, line_pair, anchor)` — all three profile-independent — so identical assignment is a theorem, not a hope. Every built dataset carries `Annotation(key="artifact_bundle_sha", value=…)`, and `validate.py` asserts (i) all four hashes match and (ii) for every `(itinerary, anchor)` present in more than one dataset, the `split` and `split_cell` annotations are byte-identical. Fail closed.

Also carry `window_start_index` / `anchor_iso` and `split_cell` as explicit annotations rather than parsing them out of `record_id` — the teammate's `record_id.rsplit("-", 1)[1]` breaks the moment ids carry route and profile structure.

---

# 7. The bake-off harness

Identical LLM (`google/gemma-3-270m`), LoRA config, optimizer, seed, **step count** (not epochs — record counts are equal by construction, another dividend of shared anchors), eval cells, generation settings.

**Pre-register the selection rule before any run** and say so in the pitch:

&gt; The winning profile is the one with the lowest `required_buffer_p80_min` MAE on the `unseen_late` cell; ties broken by action accuracy on the same cell.

Per profile, report the four leakage cells plus `line_unseen`, against four baselines:

1. **Text baseline** — same numbers serialised into the prompt, no connector.
2. **Persistence / climatology** — `clip(ctx_l1_mean_at_hour + m − usable_slack, 0, 60)`.
3. **Majority class** for the action task.
4. **Caption-only ablation** — captions kept (they carry mean and std!), series zeroed.

The caption-only ablation becomes far more informative here than in the single-scale design: **if XM's advantage survives zeroing the series, the "long context" benefit was never the shape — it was just knowing the annual mean.** That single cheap experiment directly adjudicates your hypothesis, and I would run it on S and XM before anything else optional.

Two extra rows, both cheap: **curriculum on/off** (teammate's per-station dataset as stage 1, registered in `curriculum_learning.py`), and the **cross-load** of his dataset through our bridge as the interoperability proof.

GPU cost is not the constraint: S ≈ 252 patch tokens/sample, XM ≈ 795; four runs at ~400–800 LoRA steps plus eval is on the order of **2 hours total wall clock and roughly $5–15** against a $1000 voucher. **The schedule risk is dataset build and the one long extraction, not GPU.**

---

# 8. Agentic pipeline — deltas only

The nine tiers stand (search → assessment → probe → schema map → domain adapter → miner → curator → rationale writer → validator; the bonus criterion's five verbs map to tiers 0, 1–2, 1/6, 2/4, 3/7/8, and the *rejection table* is the artifact). Three additions from the corrections:

- **New tier 5.5 — `ProfileDesigner`.** An LLM proposes candidate `(bucket, span)` pairs given the discovered source's temporal coverage; a deterministic checker enforces `n_steps ≤ 4096`, `n_steps % 4 == 0`, `span ≤ available_history − horizon`, and rejects anything violating them. This is the agentic step that *earns* the multi-timescale story: the pipeline searches over timeline configurations and the held-out cells select the winner. That is "automated search, assessment and selection" applied to the dataset's own design, not just to its sourcing — a stronger reading of the criterion than most submissions will attempt.
- **Coverage-discontinuity detection** joins the assessment tier: probing a source now reports observation-count-over-time, which is what surfaces the 2025-11-02 expansion automatically rather than by luck.
- **`convert()` is CPU-only with no network, so it cannot call an LLM.** Rationale skeletons must be generated offline and **committed as JSON inside each connector folder**. Generate them by paraphrasing the skeleton *with placeholders still in it* and verifying placeholder-set equality — ~100 calls per profile instead of 12 000, and it is then structurally impossible for the LLM to invent a number, because it never sees one.

---

# 9. Risks, fallback ladder, drop order

| # | Risk | P | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Long extraction (26 months) underruns or stalls | **high** | Launch it as a background job at H0.75; develop against 3 months; `n_steps` is config, so a shortfall shrinks L rather than killing it |
| 2 | **2025-11-02 coverage step creates a fake regime change in M/L** | **high if unhandled** | Pin all aggregates to the top-100 basis; validator asserts continuity |
| 3 | Labels degenerate (most connections just work) | high | Stratified curator quota; 5-min SQL check at H3.5; pivot to realized door-to-door delay |
| 4 | Service-group key dirty/sparse | high | Uniform `(train_type, final_destination_station)`; H0 gate |
| 5 | L overfits from ~98 % context overlap | medium | Predicted in advance, reported as effective-independent-context count; XM hedges it |
| 6 | Station/line series desynchronise by an hour | medium, **invisible** | Stay in a DST-clean range; validator cross-correlates series 1 vs 2 and asserts peak at lag 0 |
| 7 | Variable series count between profiles breaks a shared collate | low | N is fixed *within* a profile; each profile gets its own dataset class |
| 8 | Nebius `injectedFiles` ≤ 64 KiB | medium | Datasets + skeletons via S3 volume; injectedFiles carries a bootstrap script only |
| 9 | **Neither `timenet` nor `opentslm` is importable in this working copy** (verified) | certain | Pin `timenet[build]`, `timenet-connectors`, `opentslm`, `duckdb` in a reproducible env before H2 |
| 10 | Constant series → NaN on z-scoring | medium | Validator asserts `std &gt; 1e-6` per series; far more likely at L, where a daily 364-step cancel-rate series can be near-flat |

**Fallback ladder** — every rung is a shippable submission:

- **F1 (H7):** S built, validated, trained on scalar + classification with raw-template rationales. Complete submission, directly comparable to the teammate's work.
- **F2 (H12.5):** S + XM bake-off with the pre-registered rule. **This is the minimum viable version of the corrected brief** — it answers the additivity question.
- **F3 (H14.5):** all four profiles, full attribution of which scale mattered.
- **F4 (stretch):** `M-band`, budget-matched secondary comparison, full-18 XM.

**Drop order if late, decided now so nobody improvises at 4am:**
1. `M-band` and the budget-matched row
2. **L-alone** (long context still tested inside XM)
3. **M-alone** (attribution is nice-to-have; S vs XM is the headline)
4. Caption-only ablation on XM (keep it on S)
5. The prose `AnswerTask` (keep scalar + classification)
6. XM → ship S alone = F1

**Never dropped:** the shared-artifact hashing, the fail-closed validator, and one working end-to-end run.

**Hard abort on the target** (unchanged): if at H6 the miner has &lt; 60 itineraries with ≥ 20 supported days, drop transfers and fall back to the single-leg variant — 4 signals `{board_station_delay, board_line_delay, board_line_service, dest_station_delay}`, target = p80 realized arrival delay at B. The profile machinery is untouched by this; only `itineraries.py` and `labels.py` change.

Three of your premises are wrong in ways that matter. I flag them up front:

1. **`hello_world/requirements.txt` does not exist.** Only three connectors ship one (`chengsenwang/tsqa`, `physionet/ecg_qa_cot`, `physionet/sleep_edfx`). `hello_world` and `test_mean` are pure-stdlib+core and declare none.
2. **An out-of-window span is NOT rejected by default — it warns and is kept.** `warn_when_outside: bool = True`. Rejection is opt-in. This is the single most important correction to your §3 design.
3. **No shipped connector reads env vars in `__init__`.** `docs/connectors.md` claims "Configuration comes from environment variables that `__init__` reads" — no connector in the tree does this. Credentials are read by the *client library* (huggingface_hub reads `HF_TOKEN`; boto3 resolves AWS creds itself).

The `X as X` redundant-alias form is deliberate: it marks the names as re-exported for the type checker (`ty`), which otherwise flags them unused.

### `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/timenet/hello_world/connector.py`

```python
"""A synthetic, offline connector that tests every TimeF feature.

``HelloWorldConnector`` needs no network. It creates a fully deterministic dataset. The writer and
reader test suites use this dataset as a fixture for round-trip tests. The connector covers two
modalities, a shared data source, a series shared across records, and a windowed record. It also
covers a longer series. The writer tests split this longer series into chunks under a small chunk
cap. The connector covers all three annotation shapes, including one shared across records, and a
task chain.
"""

from collections.abc import Callable
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path

from jaxtyping import Float, Float64
import numpy as np
import pyarrow as pa

from timenet.connectors import BaseConnector
from timenet.dataset import TimeFDataset, TimeSeries
from timenet.dataset.axis import RegularAxis
from timenet.types import (
    Annotation,
    AnswerTask,
    ClassificationTask,
    DataSource,
    LocalizationMode,
    ScalarPredictionTask,
    TemporalLocalizationTask,
    TimeInterval,
    TimePoint,
    TimeSeriesSpec,
    ureg,
)


_SAMPLING_RATE_HZ = 16.0
_AXIS = RegularAxis.from_rate_hz(16)
_SOURCE = DataSource(data_source_type="synthetic", name="Synthetic Generator", provider="TimeNet")
_SINE = TimeSeriesSpec(
    spec_type="sine",
    name="Sine",
    unit_value=ureg.dimensionless,
    data_source=_SOURCE,
)
_COSINE = TimeSeriesSpec(
    spec_type="cosine",
    name="Cosine",
    unit_value=ureg.dimensionless,
    data_source=_SOURCE,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class HelloWorldRecording:
    """A lightweight, deterministic description of one synthetic recording."""

    index: int
    n_values: int


def _wave_values(
    fn: Callable[[Float64[np.ndarray, " time"]], Float[np.ndarray, " time"]], n: int, phase: float
) -&gt; Float[np.ndarray, " time"]:
    """Compute a closed-form wave as an array.

    The function does not use a random number generator or perform I/O.

    Args:
        fn: The wave function to apply to the angular time base, for example ``np.sin``.
        n: The number of samples.
        phase: The phase offset in radians.

    Returns:
        The wave values as a float32 ``np.ndarray``.
    """
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / _SAMPLING_RATE_HZ
    return fn(2.0 * np.pi * t + phase).astype(np.float32)


def _wave(
    fn: Callable[[Float64[np.ndarray, " time"]], Float[np.ndarray, " time"]], n: int, phase: float
) -&gt; Callable[[], pa.Array]:
    """Build a deterministic lazy loader for a closed-form wave.

    The connector uses this loader for the long series that the writer splits into chunks.

    Args:
        fn: The wave function to apply to the angular time base, for example ``np.sin``.
        n: The number of samples.
        phase: The phase offset in radians.

    Returns:
        A loader with no arguments. The loader returns the wave as a float32 Arrow array.
    """
    return lambda: pa.array(_wave_values(fn, n, phase))


class HelloWorldConnector(BaseConnector[HelloWorldRecording]):
    """A deterministic, offline demo connector for the ``timenet/hello-world`` dataset."""

    def download(self, cache_dir: Path) -&gt; list[HelloWorldRecording]:  # noqa: ARG002, PLR6301 (override: the data is synthetic, so this method needs no cache)
        """Return deterministic recording descriptions.

        This method uses no network and does not use ``cache_dir``.

        Args:
            cache_dir: Not used. The data is synthetic.

        Returns:
            One short recording and one longer recording. The writer splits the longer recording into
            chunks under a small chunk cap.
        """
        return [HelloWorldRecording(index=0, n_values=16), HelloWorldRecording(index=1, n_values=512)]

    def convert(self, raw_refs: list[HelloWorldRecording]) -&gt; TimeFDataset:
        """Build the complete dataset from the recording descriptions.

        The dataset includes every TimeF feature.

        Args:
            raw_refs: The recordings from :meth:`download`.

        Returns:
            The populated :class:`~timenet.dataset.TimeFDataset`.
        """
        dataset = TimeFDataset(metadata=self.metadata())
        short, long = raw_refs[0], raw_refs[1]

        # A series shared across two records. This uses the dedupe-by-id path.
        shared = TimeSeries.from_values(
            _wave_values(np.sin, short.n_values, phase=0.0),
            spec=_SINE,
            signal="a",
            time_axis=_AXIS,
            source_id="rec-0",
            time_series_id="ts-shared",
        )
        # An annotation shared across two records. This uses the dedupe-by-id path.
        cohort = Annotation(key="cohort", value="A", id="cohort-shared")

        # Record 0: the full recording, with two modalities, all annotation shapes, and a task chain.
        cosine = TimeSeries.from_values(
            _wave_values(np.cos, short.n_values, phase=0.0),
            spec=_COSINE,
            signal="b",
            time_axis=_AXIS,
            source_id="rec-0",
            time_series_id="ts-cos-0",
        )
        record0 = dataset.add_record(time_series=(shared, cosine), subject_ids=("subj-0",), record_id="record-0")
        # These annotations have names. The localization task below can reference them by id instead
        # of repeating the literal values.
        stimulus = Annotation(key="stimulus", span=TimePoint.seconds(0.5), id="stim-0")
        artifact = Annotation(
            key="artifact",
            span=TimeInterval.seconds(0.0, 0.25, time_series_ids=(shared.time_series_id,)),
            id="art-0",
        )
        record0.add_annotations(
            [
                Annotation(key="age", value=64, unit="years", id="age-0"),
                cohort,
                stimulus,
                artifact,
            ]
        )
        classification = ClassificationTask(target="normal", id="task-cls-0")
        dataset.add_tasks(
            record0,
            [
                classification,
                AnswerTask(
                    prompt="What rhythm?",
                    target="Normal.",
                    # Any task can carry a chain of thought. An answer task with a chain of thought is
                    # the old reasoning task.
                    rationale="The peaks repeat once per cycle at a constant interval.",
                    # The cohort annotation gives context to the model. The model does not need to
                    # produce this annotation.
                    input_annotation_ids=(cohort.id,),
                    from_tasks=(classification,),
                    id="task-answer-0",
                ),
                ScalarPredictionTask(target=60.0, unit="bpm", target_name="mean_rate", id="task-scalar-0"),
                # Localization works backward from a normal task. The query is the input, and the
                # regions are the output. Here, the task stores the answer by reference, so the target
                # is the two temporal annotations above.
                TemporalLocalizationTask(
                    prompt="Locate the stimulus and the artifact.",
                    mode=LocalizationMode.SPARSE,
                    target_annotation_ids=(stimulus.id, artifact.id),
                    id="task-localize-0",
                ),
            ],
        )

        # Record 1: this reuses the shared series and adds a longer series. The writer tests split the
        # longer series into chunks under a small chunk cap.
        long_series = TimeSeries(
            spec=_SINE,
            signal="a",
            time_axis=_AXIS,
            loader=_wave(np.sin, long.n_values, phase=1.0),
            source_id="rec-1",
            time_series_id="ts-long-1",
            n_values=long.n_values,
        )
        record1 = dataset.add_record(time_series=(shared, long_series), subject_ids=("subj-1",), record_id="record-1")
        record1.add_annotation(cohort)  # same instance and id, so the annotation is shared

        # Record 2: a windowed slice with a scoped classification task. This window covers the second
        # half of rec-0. This makes the window a genuine offset window, not a byte-identical prefix of
        # `ts-shared`. The phase offset continues the same wave, so the values match rec-0 over the
        # window.
        window_start = short.n_values // 2
        window = TimeSeries.from_values(
            _wave_values(np.sin, short.n_values - window_start, phase=2.0 * np.pi * window_start / _SAMPLING_RATE_HZ),
            spec=_SINE,
            signal="a",
            time_axis=_AXIS.at_index(window_start),
            source_id="rec-0",
            time_series_id="ts-window-2",
        )
        record2 = dataset.add_record(time_series=(window,), subject_ids=("subj-0",), record_id="record-2")
        # A scope narrows the input to a region. This task has the same task type as the whole-record
        # label above, but it supplies the window. Span times use the source recording timeline, so
        # this task's span sits inside the window's span.
        dataset.add_task(
            record2,
            ClassificationTask(
                target="onset",
                id="task-cls-2",
                scope=TimeInterval.seconds(0.5, 0.75, time_series_ids=(window.time_series_id,)),
            ),
        )
        return dataset


CONNECTOR = HelloWorldConnector
```

**What each feature demonstration is for — annotated:**

| Construct | Line region | Demonstrates | Why it's there |
|---|---|---|---|
| `_AXIS = RegularAxis.from_rate_hz(16)` | module | Integer-Hz axis built once, reused | `from_rate_hz` refuses floats; the axis is immutable so one instance is shared by every series |
| `_SOURCE` on both specs | module | One `DataSource` carried on each spec, **not** registered in a side table | `test_schema_covers_every_feature` asserts the schema dedupes it to a set of one |
| Two `TimeSeriesSpec`s (`sine`, `cosine`) | module | Two modalities in one dataset | `derive_schema()` must produce 2 specs |
| `HelloWorldRecording` frozen dataclass | 54-59 | The `TRaw` type — a *lightweight ref*, not data | The contract: `download` returns refs, `convert` parses them |
| `_wave_values` (eager) vs `_wave` (returns a closure) | 62-96 | The two `TimeSeries` construction paths | `from_values` for in-memory; `loader=` for genuinely lazy |
| `download()` returns 2 recordings, ignores `cache_dir` | 102-114 | Offline/deterministic download | `test_download_is_deterministic_and_offline` calls it with two different cache dirs and asserts equality |
| `n_values=512` on the long recording | 114 | Writer chunk-splitting | The writer test suite sets a small chunk cap and asserts this series splits |
| `shared` series attached to record-0 **and** record-1 | 131-138, 151, 209 | **Series dedupe by `time_series_id`** | Same instance, same explicit id → writer stores the bytes once |
| `cohort` annotation attached to record-0 **and** record-1 | 140, 163, 210 | **Annotation dedupe by `id`** | `test_shares_an_annotation_across_records_by_id` |
| `Annotation(key="age", value=64, unit="years")` | 162 | `AnnotationType.STATIC` + `unit=` on an annotation | A span-less scalar; the "no metadata slot" pattern |
| `Annotation(key="stimulus", span=TimePoint.seconds(0.5))` | 154 | `AnnotationType.POINT`, **unscoped** (no `time_series_ids`) | Checked against the union of series windows |
| `Annotation(key="artifact", span=TimeInterval.seconds(0.0, 0.25, time_series_ids=(...)))` | 155-159 | `AnnotationType.INTERVAL`, **scoped** to one series | Checked against that series' window only |
| Naming `stimulus` / `artifact` as locals, then `(stimulus.id, artifact.id)` | 154-159, 192 | **Never repeat an id literal** | The anatomy doc makes this a rule |
| `ClassificationTask(target="normal")` whole-record | 168 | Unscoped classification | |
| `AnswerTask(prompt=..., target=..., rationale=..., input_annotation_ids=..., from_tasks=...)` | 170-184 | Prompted QA + chain-of-thought + context annotation + **task chain** | "An answer task with a chain of thought is the old reasoning task" — CoT is not a separate task type |
| `ScalarPredictionTask(target=60.0, unit="bpm", target_name="mean_rate")` | 185 | Typed regression target with a pint-registry unit | `bpm` is a TimeNet-custom unit (`ureg.define("bpm = beat / minute")`) |
| `TemporalLocalizationTask(target_annotation_ids=(...), mode=SPARSE)` | 189-194 | **Answer by reference** instead of inline `target` | Points at the two span annotations rather than copying their spans |
| `dataset.add_tasks(record0, [...])` (batch) | 169-196 | All-or-nothing batch registration where one member derives from another **in the same batch** | Order inside the list doesn't matter; the cycle check is Kahn's algorithm |
| `_AXIS.at_index(window_start)` on record-2 | 221 | **Windowed record**: the axis origin moves into the recording | This is how a window "says so"; span times stay in the source recording timeline |
| `ClassificationTask(..., scope=TimeInterval.seconds(0.5, 0.75, ...))` on record-2 | 231-235 | Scoped classification — *same class* as the whole-record one | The type says what kind of answer; `scope` says over what region |
| `dataset.add_task(...)` singular | 229 | The singular form (a batch of one) | |
| `CONNECTOR = HelloWorldConnector` | 240 | The discovery hook | `discovery.resolve()` reads exactly this name |

### `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/timenet/hello_world/dataset.yaml`

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://docs.timenet.ai/schemas/dataset-card-v1.schema.json
# Dataset Card for the timenet/hello-world connector: the single source for this dataset's metadata.
# The connector loads it via DatasetMetadata.from_yaml(); the builder compiles it plus the schema
# derived from the data into manifest.json. The connector package lives at datasets/&lt;org&gt;/&lt;name&gt;/.
yaml_schema_version: 1
dataset_id: timenet/hello-world
dataset_version: 1.0.0
name: Hello World
description: A synthetic demo dataset exercising every TimeF feature.
license: CC-BY-4.0
domains:
  - general
tags:
  - demo
  - synthetic
```

Note `dataset_id: timenet/hello-world` (hyphen) maps to folder `timenet/hello_world` (underscore).

### `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/timenet/hello_world/tests/__init__.py`

Empty file (0 bytes). Required so `discovery.available()` can skip it by package-name segment, and so pytest treats the tests as a package.

### `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/timenet/hello_world/tests/test_connector.py`

```python
from pathlib import Path

from timenet.connectors import BaseConnector
from timenet.dataset import TimeFDataset
from timenet.testing import assert_datasets_equal
from timenet.types import (
    AnnotationType,
    AnswerTask,
    ClassificationTask,
    DataSource,
    ScalarPredictionTask,
    TemporalLocalizationTask,
)
from timenet_connectors.datasets.timenet.hello_world import HelloWorldConnector


def _convert() -&gt; TimeFDataset:
    connector = HelloWorldConnector()
    return connector.convert(connector.download(Path("cache")))


def test_is_a_connector():
    assert isinstance(HelloWorldConnector(), BaseConnector)


def test_metadata():
    assert HelloWorldConnector().metadata().dataset_id == "timenet/hello-world"


def test_download_is_deterministic_and_offline():
    connector = HelloWorldConnector()
    assert connector.download(Path("cache-a")) == connector.download(Path("cache-b"))


def test_convert_is_deterministic():
    assert_datasets_equal(_convert(), _convert())


def test_schema_covers_every_feature():
    schema = _convert().derive_schema()
    assert len(schema.time_series_specs) == 2  # sine + cosine
    # one source, carried on each spec rather than registered once in a side table
    assert {s.data_source for s in schema.time_series_specs} == {
        DataSource(data_source_type="synthetic", name="Synthetic Generator", provider="TimeNet")
    }
    annotation_types = {a.annotation_type for a in schema.annotations}
    assert annotation_types == {AnnotationType.STATIC, AnnotationType.POINT, AnnotationType.INTERVAL}
    assert set(schema.tasks) == {ClassificationTask, AnswerTask, ScalarPredictionTask, TemporalLocalizationTask}


def test_shares_a_series_across_records_by_id():
    dataset = _convert()
    series_by_id: dict[str, int] = {}
    for record in dataset.records:
        for ts in record.time_series:
            series_by_id[ts.time_series_id] = series_by_id.get(ts.time_series_id, 0) + 1
    assert any(count &gt;= 2 for count in series_by_id.values()), "expected a series shared across records"


def test_shares_an_annotation_across_records_by_id():
    dataset = _convert()
    ann_ids: dict[str, int] = {}
    for record in dataset.records:
        for ann in record.annotations:
            ann_ids[ann.id] = ann_ids.get(ann.id, 0) + 1
    assert any(count &gt;= 2 for count in ann_ids.values()), "expected an annotation shared across records"


def test_has_a_task_chain():
    dataset = _convert()
    assert any(task.from_tasks for task in dataset.tasks), "expected a task derived via from_tasks"
```

**Test-file conventions worth copying literally:**
- No `TestX` classes, no fixtures file, no docstrings on tests — plain functions with speaking names.
- `_convert()` helper at the top; every test calls it fresh.
- `test_is_a_connector` / `test_metadata` are the boilerplate pair every connector test module has.
- `assert_datasets_equal` from `timenet.testing` is the determinism oracle. Its source is at `/…/packages/timenet/src/timenet/testing/builders.py` (line 211 compares `subject_ids`).

```python
"""The test-mean connector package: its connector implementation and dataset card."""
```

### `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/timenet/test_mean/connector.py`

```python
"""Synthetic offline connector for the ``timenet/test-mean`` classification demo.

Each record is one noisy signal. The signal is a constant offset plus Gaussian noise. The label is
``above_zero`` or ``below_zero`` by the sign of the offset. A classifier can recover the sign from
simple summary features. Offsets alternate by index, so the two-class set is exactly balanced. Every
value is seeded, so the dataset is deterministic and needs no network. The dataset has no raw source.
``download`` returns nothing and ``convert`` builds every record. This connector backs the end-to-end
training example.
"""

from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

from timenet.connectors import BaseConnector
from timenet.dataset import TimeFDataset, TimeSeries
from timenet.dataset.axis import RegularAxis
from timenet.types import ClassificationTask, DataSource, TimeSeriesSpec, ureg


_N_RECORDS = 1000
_LENGTH = 64
_SAMPLING_RATE_HZ = 16.0
_NOISE_STD = 0.5
_SEED = 20260715  # fixed base seed keeps every build byte-for-byte identical

_SOURCE = DataSource(data_source_type="synthetic", name="Synthetic Generator", provider="TimeNet")
_SIGNAL = TimeSeriesSpec(
    spec_type="signal",
    name="Signal",
    unit_value=ureg.dimensionless,
    data_source=_SOURCE,
)


class TestMeanConnector(BaseConnector[None]):
    """A deterministic, offline connector for the ``timenet/test-mean`` classification demo.

    Synthetic: :meth:`download` returns no references and :meth:`convert` generates every record directly.
    """

    def download(self, cache_dir: Path) -&gt; list[None]:  # noqa: ARG002, PLR6301 (synthetic: nothing to download)
        """Return no references; the data is synthetic and built entirely in :meth:`convert`.

        Args:
            cache_dir: Unused; nothing is fetched.

        Returns:
            An empty list.
        """
        return []

    def convert(self, raw_refs: list[None]) -&gt; TimeFDataset:  # noqa: ARG002 (synthetic: nothing to convert)
        """Generate the balanced set of labeled, single-signal records.

        Args:
            raw_refs: Unused; the data is synthetic (``download`` returns nothing).

        Returns:
            The populated dataset: one single-signal series and one ``ClassificationTask`` per record.
        """
        dataset = TimeFDataset(metadata=self.metadata())
        rng = np.random.default_rng(_SEED)
        for index in range(_N_RECORDS):
            offset = float(rng.uniform(0.3, 1.5)) * (1.0 if index % 2 == 0 else -1.0)
            values = offset + rng.normal(0.0, _NOISE_STD, _LENGTH)
            series = TimeSeries.from_values(
                values,
                spec=_SIGNAL,
                signal="signal",
                time_axis=RegularAxis.from_rate_hz(16),
                source_id=f"rec-{index}",
                time_series_id=f"ts-{index}",
            )
            record = dataset.add_record(time_series=(series,), record_id=f"record-{index}")
            label = "above_zero" if offset &gt; 0 else "below_zero"
            dataset.add_task(record, ClassificationTask(target=label, id=f"task-{index}"))
        return dataset


CONNECTOR = TestMeanConnector
```

### `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/timenet/test_mean/dataset.yaml`

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://docs.timenet.ai/schemas/dataset-card-v1.schema.json
# Dataset Card for the timenet/test-mean connector: the single source for this dataset's metadata.
# The connector loads it via DatasetMetadata.from_yaml(); the builder compiles it plus the schema
# derived from the data into manifest.json. The connector package lives at datasets/&lt;org&gt;/&lt;name&gt;/.

dataset_version: 1.0.0
name: Test Mean
description: &gt;-
  A synthetic binary-classification demo: each record is one noisy signal labeled by whether its mean
  is above or below zero. Balanced and deterministic; backs the end-to-end training example.

  - general

  - synthetic
  - classification
```

### `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/timenet/test_mean/tests/__init__.py`

### `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/timenet/test_mean/tests/test_connector.py`

```python
from collections import Counter
from pathlib import Path

from timenet.connectors import BaseConnector
from timenet.dataset import TimeFDataset
from timenet.testing import assert_datasets_equal
from timenet.types import ClassificationTask
from timenet_connectors.datasets.timenet.test_mean import TestMeanConnector


def _convert() -&gt; TimeFDataset:
    return TestMeanConnector().convert([])


def _labels_by_record(dataset: TimeFDataset) -&gt; dict[str, str | None]:
    return {
        record_id: task.target
        for task in dataset.tasks
        if isinstance(task, ClassificationTask)
        for record_id in task.record_ids
    }


def test_is_a_connector():
    assert isinstance(TestMeanConnector(), BaseConnector)


def test_metadata():
    assert TestMeanConnector().metadata().dataset_id == "timenet/test-mean"


def test_download_defaults_to_no_op():
    # Synthetic connector: it generates everything in convert and inherits the no-op download.
    assert TestMeanConnector().download(Path("cache")) == []


def test_convert_is_deterministic():
    assert_datasets_equal(_convert(), _convert())


def test_has_balanced_binary_labels():
    labels = Counter(_labels_by_record(_convert()).values())
    assert labels == Counter({"above_zero": 500, "below_zero": 500})


def test_target_has_no_schema():
    assert all(task.target_schema is None for task in _convert().tasks_of(ClassificationTask))


def test_every_record_is_a_single_signal():
    dataset = _convert()
    assert len(dataset.records) == 1000
    for record in dataset.records:
        assert len(record.time_series) == 1


def test_label_agrees_with_signal_mean():
    # The task is solvable from the data: a mean-based classifier would recover every label.
    dataset = _convert()
    labels = _labels_by_record(dataset)
    for record in dataset.records:
        mean = float(record.time_series[0].to_numpy().mean())
        expected = "above_zero" if mean &gt; 0 else "below_zero"
        assert labels[record.record_id] == expected
```

**Floor vs. ceiling, the delta:** the minimum viable connector is `__init__.py` + `connector.py` + `dataset.yaml` + `tests/`. No annotations at all, one task per record, one series per record, no `subject_ids`, no spans, no `register_annotations`, no `requirements.txt`. `test_label_agrees_with_signal_mean` is the one test idea worth stealing: **assert the task is actually solvable from the data you shipped.**

# §2. The `add-dataset-connector` agent skill, in full

## 2.1 `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/.agents/skills/add-dataset-connector/SKILL.md` — verbatim, in full

```markdown
---
name: add-dataset-connector
description: Use when adding a new TimeNet dataset connector, i.e. converting an external dataset (from a HuggingFace repo, PhysioNet, or another source given by a link or reference) into the TimeF format. Guides exploring the source, choosing the task type and example-row shape, confirming the plan, then implementing the connector folder that reuses the HuggingFace or PhysioNet base connectors.
---

# Adding a dataset connector

A connector fetches a dataset's raw source and converts it into a `TimeFDataset`. It implements the
`BaseConnector` contract (in the `timenet` package) and lives in `timenet-connectors`. The engine drives
it `download -&gt; convert -&gt; derive_schema -&gt; store`; `timenet-build build &lt;id&gt;` runs that pipeline and
writes the result into a registry.

Reuse a base connector wherever the source allows: `BaseHuggingFaceConnector` for Hub datasets,
`BasePhysioNetConnector` for PhysioNet/WFDB records. Write a raw `BaseConnector` subclass only when
neither fits.

Read `references/connector-anatomy.md` for the contract, the base-connector APIs, the task types, the
folder layout, and a fully worked example before implementing.

## Workflow

Create one task per step. Do the exploration and design first, get sign-off at the gate, then build.

### 1. Take the input and derive the id
Start from the link or reference the user gives (a HuggingFace dataset URL, a PhysioNet page, etc.).
Derive an `org/name` id: lowercase, hyphens allowed in the leaf. On disk hyphens become underscores, so
`physionet/ecg-qa-cot` maps to `datasets/physionet/ecg_qa_cot/`. The id shape is validated as
`^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$`, and no segment may start with `.`.

### 2. Explore the source structure
Understand the raw data before designing anything:
- Fields/columns and their types; which one holds the series values and how they are encoded (JSON
  list, list-of-lists for multivariate, WFDB record, etc.).
- Series shape: univariate vs multivariate, number of signals, length.
- Sampling rate and physical units (Hz, seconds, mV, dimensionless).
- Labels, questions/answers, rationales, splits, and any per-row metadata.

For HuggingFace, the Hub auto-converts public datasets to parquet on the `refs/convert/parquet` branch;
inspect columns via the dataset viewer or the datasets-server API. For PhysioNet, inspect the archive
layout and a WFDB header (`fs`, `sig_len`, `sig_name`).

### 3. Pick the base connector
- Hub dataset: subclass `BaseHuggingFaceConnector`, set `HF_REPO`, inherit `download`, implement only
  `convert`.
- PhysioNet/WFDB: subclass `BasePhysioNetConnector`, implement `download` (use `ensure_archive` /
  `download_files` from `timenet_connectors.download`) and `convert` (use `_read_header` / `_lead_loader`).
- Neither: subclass `BaseConnector[TRaw]` and implement `download` + `convert` yourself.

### 4. Determine the task and sketch an example row
- Choose one built-in `Task` by the *kind of answer* the dataset supervises: `ClassificationTask` (a
  category, whole-record or over a `scope`), `AnswerTask` (free text; a caption without a `prompt`),
  `ScalarPredictionTask` (a number with a unit), `TemporalLocalizationTask` (regions to find),
  `ForecastingTask`, `TSEditingTask`, `TSGenerationTask`, or `TSCorrespondenceTask`. Any of them can carry
  a `rationale`, so a chain-of-thought dataset is not a separate type.
- Sketch one `Record`: its `TimeSeries` signal(s) with their `spec` and `time_axis`;
  the annotations you'll attach; and the task payload. Concrete values, not placeholders.

### 5. HARD GATE: confirm with the user

**STOP. Do not write any connector code yet.** Present, and wait for explicit approval:
- the proposed `org/name` id,
- the source and the base connector you'll reuse,
- the task type, and
- a concrete example-row sketch (signals + units + annotations + task payload).

Only continue once the user confirms. If they change the task or shape, revise the sketch and re-confirm.

### 6. Implement the connector folder
Create `packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/&lt;org&gt;/&lt;name&gt;/` with:
- `dataset.yaml`, the dataset card: `yaml_schema_version`, `dataset_id`, `dataset_version`, `name`,
  `description`, `license`, `domains`, `tags`.
- `connector.py`, the `BaseConnector` subclass, ending with a module-level `CONNECTOR = &lt;YourClass&gt;`.
- `__init__.py`, re-exporting `CONNECTOR` (and the class) from `connector.py`.
- `requirements.txt`, when the connector needs a library outside `timenet-connectors`' core
  dependencies. Import it lazily inside the connector. If it is missing, raise a clear error. A build
  installs it into the environment that the build runs in. The lazy import keeps `--no-isolation`
  usable while you write the connector.

Add the org namespace `__init__.py` if the org is new. Keep `download` I/O-only and `convert` CPU-only
with lazy value loaders (never materialize arrays in `convert`). See the worked example in
`references/connector-anatomy.md`.

### 7. Verify
- Add a fixture-based test in `&lt;org&gt;/&lt;name&gt;/tests/`, mirroring the one at
  `datasets/chengsenwang/tsqa/tests/test_connector.py`: check in a tiny sample of the raw shape and call
  `convert()` on it directly (no network). The `TIMENET_TESTING` / `TIMENET_ROW_LIMIT` env vars mentioned
  in some docs are **not implemented**, so don't rely on them.
- If you added or changed a `requirements.txt`, re-run `make sync` so the new library lands in your own
  environment. Otherwise `ty` reports your lazy import as unresolved and the connector test can't run.
- Round-trip end to end: `uv run timenet-build build &lt;id&gt; --out &lt;tmp-dir&gt;`, then
  `TimeNet(registry="&lt;tmp-dir&gt;").load("&lt;id&gt;").describe()`.
- Run `make check` and `make test`, and state which checks you ran (per AGENTS.md).

## Further reading

`references/connector-anatomy.md` in this skill. Repo docs: `docs/connectors.md` and `docs/build.md`
(design proposal; where they describe a flat-file `datasets/&lt;org&gt;/&lt;name&gt;.py` layout, the real code uses
the folder-package layout above, so follow the code).
```

Two notes for you specifically:
- **Step 5 is a hard gate.** If you drive this with an agent, it will stop and demand sign-off on id + source + base + task type + example row before writing code.
- The closing note about `docs/connectors.md` describing a **flat-file layout is now stale** — the current `/…/docs/connectors.md` (lines 71-80) already describes the folder-package layout correctly. The skill's warning is a leftover.

## 2.2 `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gaurav-Downloads-zurich-lates-temporal-ai-error404-ai/6f11fbbe-b41b-4ac6-96cf-af296c485bf7/scratchpad/TimeNet/.agents/skills/add-dataset-connector/references/connector-anatomy.md` — verbatim, in full

````markdown
# Connector anatomy

Deep reference for the `add-dataset-connector` skill. Everything here is verified against the code on
this branch. Anchor files:

- Contract: `packages/timenet/src/timenet/connectors/base.py`
- Bases: `packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/bases/{huggingface,physionet}.py`
- Discovery: `packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/discovery.py`
- Build CLI: `packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/builder/cli.py`
- Worked examples: the `chengsenwang/tsqa`, `physionet/ecg_qa_cot`, and `timenet/hello_world` connectors

## The `BaseConnector` contract

`BaseConnector(ABC, Generic[TRaw])` in `timenet.connectors`. `TRaw` is whatever `download` hands to
`convert` (a dict per Hub row, a dataclass ref per PhysioNet record, etc.).

- `download(self, cache_dir: Path) -&gt; list[TRaw]` (abstract): fetch/discover raw source files, return
  lightweight refs. I/O only, no parsing, idempotent for a given `cache_dir`.
- `convert(self, raw_refs: list[TRaw]) -&gt; TimeFDataset` (abstract): parse refs into a `TimeFDataset`.
  CPU only, no network.
- `metadata(self) -&gt; DatasetMetadata` (**concrete**, do not override): loads and validates the card via
  `DatasetMetadata.from_yaml`. By convention the card is `dataset.yaml` beside the connector module;
  set the `CARD` class var to point elsewhere. (Some docs call `metadata` abstract; it isn't.)
- `store(...)` (concrete): derives the schema if missing and streams the dataset through `TimeFWriter`.
  Most connectors never override it.

Connectors take **no constructor arguments** (configuration comes from the environment). End with a
module-level `CONNECTOR = &lt;YourClass&gt;`.

## Discovery and the folder layout

A connector is a package folder, not a flat file:

```
packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/&lt;org&gt;/&lt;name&gt;/
  __init__.py      # re-exports CONNECTOR (and the class) from connector.py
  connector.py     # the BaseConnector subclass; ends with CONNECTOR = &lt;YourClass&gt;
  dataset.yaml     # the dataset card, read by metadata()
  requirements.txt # the libraries this connector needs, installed into the
                   # environment its build runs in (optional)
```

`discovery.resolve(dataset_id)` imports only the one module and reads its `CONNECTOR`.
`discovery._module_name` maps the id to the module path: org lowercased, leaf hyphens to underscores, so
`chengsenwang/tsqa -&gt; ...datasets.chengsenwang.tsqa` and `physionet/ecg-qa-cot -&gt; ...datasets.physionet.ecg_qa_cot`.
The org folder needs its own `__init__.py` (a namespace package that exposes no `CONNECTOR`).

## The dataset card (`dataset.yaml`)

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://docs.timenet.ai/schemas/dataset-card-v1.schema.json
yaml_schema_version: 1
dataset_id: chengsenwang/tsqa
dataset_version: 1.0.0
name: TSQA
description: "Time-series question answering: a series plus a question/answer per record."
license: Apache-2.0
domains:
  - general
tags:
  - qa
  - time-series
  - huggingface
```

`dataset_id` must equal the id the connector is built under. `license` must be a valid `License`;
`domains` valid `Domain` values. The card is validated on load (needs the `build` extra, which
`timenet[build]` pulls in); errors raise `TimeNetInvalidCardError`.

## Base connectors to reuse

### `BaseHuggingFaceConnector` (`bases/huggingface.py`)
`BaseHuggingFaceConnector(BaseConnector[dict[str, Any]])`. Set `HF_REPO` to the external Hub repo id
and implement `convert`. `download` is inherited: it reads the Hub's auto-converted parquet on
`refs/convert/parquet` and returns one dict per row, so any source format is handled uniformly.
The connector imports `huggingface_hub` lazily and declares it in its `requirements.txt`. The lazy
import keeps `--no-isolation` usable while you write the connector. `HF_TOKEN` is read from the
environment, so gated datasets work. Fully private repos have no auto-parquet ref and aren't supported.

### `BasePhysioNetConnector` (`bases/physionet.py`)
`BasePhysioNetConnector(BaseConnector[TRaw])`. Implement `download` and `convert`. Its two helpers read
records through `wfdb`, which the base imports lazily so that `--no-isolation` stays usable while you
write the connector:
- `_read_header(record_base)`: WFDB header (`fs`, `sig_len`, `sig_name`), no signal decode.
- `_lead_loader(record_base, lead_idx) -&gt; Callable[[], pa.Array]`: lazy float32 loader for one lead's
  physical signal.

The base does not fetch the archive. Call `ensure_archive` / `download_files` from
`timenet_connectors.download`. The connector's `requirements.txt` then declares `wfdb` plus whatever
its download path needs. `physionet/ecg_qa_cot` pulls PTB-XL from `s3://physionet-open/`, which goes
through boto3, so it names `boto3` too.

## Building the dataset in `convert`

Populate a `TimeFDataset` (`from timenet.dataset import TimeFDataset, TimeSeries`):

- `TimeSeries.from_values(values, *, spec, signal, time_axis, source_id=None, time_series_id=None)`
  is the shortcut when you already hold the values in memory: it wraps them in a **float32** loader and
  takes `n_values` from the array's own length. When the source has one arbitrary time offset per point,
  use `TimeSeries.from_irregular(values, *, time_offsets_us, spec, signal, ...)` instead, which derives
  the axis from the stream. Use the raw `TimeSeries(..., loader=&lt;Callable[[], pa.Array]&gt;, ...)`
  constructor only for genuinely lazy sources (files, remote shards). `time_series_id` is the dedupe key:
  reuse the same id (and the same `TimeSeries`) to share one series across records.
- `spec` is a `TimeSeriesSpec(spec_type=..., name=..., unit_value=ureg.&lt;unit&gt;, data_source=...)`.
  Units come from the shared pint registry `ureg` (`from timenet.types import ureg`). Optional
  `data_source=DataSource(data_source_type=..., name=..., provider=...)`.
- `record = dataset.add_record(time_series=&lt;tuple of TimeSeries&gt;, record_id=...)`. A windowed record
  says so through its axis: `RegularAxis.at_index(...)` moves the origin into the recording.
- `record.add_annotation(Annotation(key=..., value=..., id=...))` attaches one and returns it;
  `record.add_annotations([...])` takes an iterable and returns a tuple. One class: its shape comes from
  its `span`. No span means whole-record; `span=TimePoint.seconds(...)` a time offset;
  `span=TimeInterval.seconds(...)` a region.
- `dataset.add_task(record, &lt;Task&gt;(...))` registers one and returns it; `dataset.add_tasks(record, [...])`
  takes an iterable and registers the batch all-or-nothing. Set `scope` and `from_tasks` on the task
  itself, not the call; a batch may derive from its own members in any order.
- Name any annotation or task you reference later and read its `id` off it. Never repeat an id literal in
  `input_annotation_ids`, `target_annotation_ids`, or `from_tasks`.

## Task types (`timenet.types.tasks`)

The task **class** is the type tag (used by `search(task=...)`); the instance carries the payload.

Every task shares one frame on the `Task` base — `record_ids`, `prompt`, `scope` (a `Span` narrowing the
input), `input_annotation_ids`, `target` / `target_annotation_ids`, `rationale`, `from_tasks` — so the
type only says what *kind* of answer it is.

| Task | Answer | Extra payload |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ClassificationTask` | `target: str` (a label) | optional `target_schema` |
| `AnswerTask` | `target: str` (free text; a caption when there is no `prompt`) | — |
| `ScalarPredictionTask` | `target: float` | optional `unit`, `target_name` |
| `TemporalLocalizationTask` | `target: tuple[Span, ...]` | `mode` (`SPARSE` / `EXHAUSTIVE`) |
| `ForecastingTask` | the produced series | `context_record_ids`, `target_record_id` |
| `TSEditingTask` | the produced series | `source_record_id`, `target_record_id` |
| `TSGenerationTask` | the produced series | `target_record_id` |
| `TSCorrespondenceTask` | `target: tuple[str, ...]` (record ids) | `candidate_record_ids` |

The three series-output tasks set `answer_is_record` and locate their answer by record id instead of
filling `target`. Every other task needs exactly one of `target` or `target_annotation_ids` (the latter
points at stored annotations instead of copying them into the task row); `add_task` enforces that, plus
the bounds of every `Span` the task carries.

## Worked example: `chengsenwang/tsqa` (HuggingFace, QA)

`connector.py`:

```python
import json
from typing import Any

from timenet.dataset import TimeFDataset, TimeSeries
from timenet.dataset.axis import OrdinalAxis
from timenet.types import Annotation, AnswerTask, TimeSeriesSpec, ureg
from timenet_connectors.bases.huggingface import BaseHuggingFaceConnector

_SPEC = TimeSeriesSpec(
    spec_type="tsqa_series",
    name="TSQA Series",
    unit_value=ureg.dimensionless,
)

class TSQAConnector(BaseHuggingFaceConnector):
    """Connector for the TSQA time-series QA dataset."""

    HF_REPO = "ChengsenWang/TSQA"  # external Hub repo id, keeps its own casing

    def convert(self, raw_refs: list[dict[str, Any]]) -&gt; TimeFDataset:
        dataset = TimeFDataset(metadata=self.metadata())
        for index, row in enumerate(raw_refs):
            series = json.loads(row["Series"])
            signals = series if series and isinstance(series[0], list) else [series]
            time_series = tuple(
                TimeSeries.from_values(
                    values,
                    spec=_SPEC,
                    signal=f"c{signal}",
                    time_axis=OrdinalAxis(),
                    time_series_id=f"row-{index}-c{signal}",
                )
                for signal, values in enumerate(signals)
            )
            record = dataset.add_record(time_series=time_series, record_id=f"row-{index}")
            record.add_annotation(Annotation(key="task", value=row["Task"], id=f"task-{index}"))
            if row.get("Label"):
                record.add_annotation(Annotation(key="label", value=row["Label"], id=f"label-{index}"))
            dataset.add_task(record, AnswerTask(prompt=row["Question"], target=row["Answer"], id=f"qa-{index}"))
        return dataset

CONNECTOR = TSQAConnector
```

`__init__.py`:

```python
from timenet_connectors.datasets.chengsenwang.tsqa.connector import (
    CONNECTOR as CONNECTOR,
    TSQAConnector as TSQAConnector,
)
```

## PhysioNet notes: `physionet/ecg_qa_cot`

Subclasses `BasePhysioNetConnector[EcgQaCotRef]` where `EcgQaCotRef` is a frozen dataclass ref.
`download` calls `ensure_archive` / `download_files` and returns refs; `convert` shares the 12-lead
ECG across rows on the same recording (`leads_by_ecg` cache keyed by a stable `time_series_id`), attaches
whole-record `Annotation`s (split, question_type, template_id, clinical_context, answer_options), and adds a
`AnswerTask(prompt=..., rationale=&lt;CoT&gt;, target=&lt;label&gt;)`. `_leads_for` reads the WFDB header for
`fs`/`sig_len`/`sig_name` and builds one lazy `TimeSeries` per lead. See its `connector.py` for the full
pattern, including sharing a series across many records.

## Fixture-based test pattern

Tests live beside their connector, in `&lt;org&gt;/&lt;name&gt;/tests/`. Mirror the `chengsenwang/tsqa` one at
`packages/timenet-connectors/src/timenet_connectors/datasets/chengsenwang/tsqa/tests/test_connector.py`:
check a tiny raw sample into `tests/fixtures/`, then call `convert()` on it directly and assert on
records, tasks, annotations, and parsed values. No network, no env-var toggles.
````

**One inaccuracy in the anatomy doc:** it says the fixture goes into `tests/fixtures/`. The actual tsqa test inlines `_FIXTURE_ROWS` as a module-level Python list in `test_connector.py` — there is no `fixtures/` directory anywhere in the repo. Follow the code: inline the fixture.
